Theorem 211.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: The formal_languages operator L associated with convergence analysis theory satisfies the maximum principle with constant 671. Its spectrum is contained in the disk of radius 520 with 4 eigenvalues on the boundary.

Proof: We construct the required map explicitly. Starting from the convergence analysis condition on formal_languages structure S, we define the operator T by T(x) = Σ c_k x^k where |c_k| ≤ 671. The convergence follows from the convergence analysis estimate ||T^n|| ≤ 520. QED.

Theorem 211.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: For any formal_languages module M satisfying the structural decomposition hypothesis, the class [M] in K_0(R) has order dividing 250. The projective resolution has length at most 607 with Betti numbers bounded by 4.

Proof: By the structural decomposition principle applied to formal_languages theory, we decompose the problem into 250 independent subproblems. Thus, each subproblem has a unique solution by the structural decomposition fixed-point theorem with constant 607. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result with the bound O(4/n). QED.

Statement: Given a formal_languages algebra A over field F with conservation law conditions, the decomposition A = A_1 ⊕ A_2 yields invariant subspaces with dimension at most 77. The spectral radius satisfies ρ(T) ≤ 470/1.

Proof: By the conservation law principle applied to formal_languages theory, we decompose the problem into 77 independent subproblems. Thus, each subproblem has a unique solution by the conservation law fixed-point theorem with constant 470. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result with the bound O(1/n). QED.

Theorem 211.999 (Entropy Bound)

Statement: Under the entropy bound assumption on formal_languages structure S, the characteristic class c_k(S) vanishes for k > 710. The remaining classes generate a ring of rank 184 with relations in degree 3.

Proof: By the entropy bound decomposition theorem, the relevant homology group H_710(S, ≠) vanishes for dimension 710. The extension on the ambient space provides the required map, and the obstruction class in H^184(S, F) is trivial. QED.

Theorem 211.1000 (Existence and Uniqueness)

Statement: The formal_languages variety V defined by existence and uniqueness constraints admits a stratification into 906 smooth components. Each component has Euler characteristic χ ≤ 157 and intersection form of rank 3.

Proof: The proof follows by induction on the dimension. The base case of dimension 906 is immediate from the existence and uniqueness property. For the inductive step, we decompose the formal_languages structure using the existence and uniqueness theorem, obtaining subproblems of dimension at most 157. Each subproblem has a solution by the existence and uniqueness fixed-point theorem with constant 3. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result. QED.
